# Vertical Platforms — Education

## 🆕 `p751` — 🟢 **THE SCHEDULING PLATFORM SHELF: `4` DEPLOYABLE SYSTEMS, `3` OF THEM PERMISSIVE. 🟢 CERN'S ROOM-BOOKING AND EVENT PLATFORM IS **MIT** AT `2,128★` AND THIS BASE HELD IT AT `0` MENTIONS. 🔴 THE SCHOOL-PORTAL LAYER THAT EVERY EUROPEAN PARENT USES IS ENTIRELY PROPRIETARY — AND THAT IS THE INTEGRATION OPPORTUNITY, NOT A BLOCKER.**

**Pass p751 · verified 2026-11-10.** Deployable systems for the scheduling, matching and resource-allocation register. 🔵 **The division of labour on this shelf: `UniTime` and `Indico` are systems a client may already run; `Timefold` is an engine you deploy *inside* something; the portals are systems you integrate *with* and cannot adopt.**

These are **proprietary** platforms with large national install bases. A studio cannot adopt them; it integrates with them — and for four of them a **permissive, maintained MCP adapter already exists** (see [`../agents/top.md`](../agents/top.md), `T444`).

🟢 **The asymmetry is the commercial point.** In EMEA the last mile to a parent-facing scheduling assistant is **already written and MIT-licensed**. In North America and LATAM it is not, and it is the billable part.

### 🔴 `T431` — CPAL-1.0 is an OSI licence that still breaks a white-label, and this base had no scope for that shape

`ChildMindInstitute/mindlogger-app` ships the **Common Public Attribution License 1.0** — a `27,886 B`
payload whose own header points at the OSI listing. 🟢 **It is genuinely open source.** 🔴 **And it
carries two clauses a studio must price in before the pitch:**

1. **Attribution.** CPAL exists to require a *visible* attribution — the original author's name or logo
   displayed in the user interface of every derivative. **A client expecting their own branding on a
   student-facing wellbeing app cannot have it on this codebase without negotiating.**
2. **Network use.** Like AGPL, deploying it as a service counts as distribution.

🔵 **Why this matters more than the licence itself:** `p749` recorded `NOASSERTION` as having two causes
(`T419`); this is a **third** — *a licence GitHub's classifier does not recognise at all*. The shelf
rule stays the same and gets stronger: **read the payload, and read its first paragraph, not just its
title block** (`T435`).

### 🔴 Measured nil — recorded because silence looks exactly like coverage

🔴 **There is no integrated, open-source student-safety or student-success platform.** The commercial
category — a system that watches school-issued accounts and devices, scores risk and alerts an adult —
is owned outright by proprietary vendors (Gaggle, GoGuardian, Securly, Bark, Lightspeed; EAB Navigate
and Civitas on the retention side). The open-source searches that would find a counterpart return:
`wellbeing survey platform` **`6`** repositories (top result `1★`); `student counseling management`
`105` (top result `4★`); `behavior intervention school` `16`; `safeguarding school` **`31`, and its
highest-starred result at `3★` is a USB-attack C2 tool that merely contains the word**. 🟢 **Every
component needed to assemble the real thing is on this page and on
[`../repos/foundations.md`](../repos/foundations.md), permissively licensed or deployable; the
integrated product does not exist.** See `P251` and `P252`.

### 🔴 Enumerated and NOT filed — with the reason

- [`formbricks/formbricks`](https://github.com/formbricks/formbricks) — `13,231★`, pushed `2026-11-10`, index `NOASSERTION`. A strong survey platform on capability, **not filed on licence shape**: the root `LICENSE` is a `35,154 B` composite (sha256₁₂ `b32292faef23`) that licenses `apps/web/modules/ee` under a **separate enterprise licence** and only some `packages/*` under MIT. 🔵 Filed here rather than shelved so a later pass can decide whether a per-directory composite is acceptable to a client's legal review — the `T435` shape.
- [`ohcnetwork/care`](https://github.com/ohcnetwork/care) — **MIT**, `421★`, pushed `2026-11-10`. Open Healthcare Network's patient-care platform. On-topic for a campus *health* service, **not filed** because it belongs to the healthcare KB rather than this one; recorded so the boundary is deliberate.
- `apereo/student-success-plan` — 🔴 **`422`.** See `T434`: Apereo's Student Success Plan is not reachable on GitHub at all, which is an instrument boundary rather than evidence it does not exist.

### 🔴 `T419` — `NOASSERTION` has two distinct causes in this tier and they must not be conflated

Two rows above are indexed `NOASSERTION`, for completely different reasons, and a shelf that treats
them alike will mis-advise a client:

1. **The grant is present and self-declares SPDX, but the title block is the project name.**
   `blindpandas/bookworm` opens `Bookworm` / `Copyright (c) 2026 …` / `SPDX-License-Identifier: GPL-2.0-or-later`.
   🟢 **The licence is knowable with certainty from the payload** — GitHub's classifier simply could not
   see it. This is `p748`'s `T412` failure mode, recurring on a different estate.
2. **The grant is a composite of vendored third-party licences with no single primary.**
   [`ryersondmp/sa11y`](https://github.com/ryersondmp/sa11y) (`338★`) ships a `21,244 B` `LICENSE.md`
   naming **MIT `×25`** and **ISC `×3`**. The project's own grant is MIT; the bulk is its dependencies'.
   🔵 Filed here rather than as a platform row: Sa11y is an *in-page* authoring checker, which makes it
   a strong candidate for `verticals` at a later pass once somebody decides whether the composite
   payload is acceptable to a client's legal review.

### 🔴 `T420` — the grant of the single most important program in this tier is at a path nobody probes, and it is not a clean SPDX id

`nvaccess/nvda` publishes its licence at lowercase **`copying.txt`** (`53,408 B`). It is absent from
all `6` capitalised paths this pass probes, and `COPYING.txt` returns `404` — **the case matters**.
The payload's own first substantive line:

> *NVDA is available under the GNU General Public License version 2 or later, **with two special exceptions**.*

The exceptions are named as **"Non-GPL Components in Plugins and Drivers"** and **"Microsoft
Distributable Code"**, and the file states it is authoritative over per-file headers.
🔴 **So the honest statement to a client is "GPL-2.0-or-later with two exceptions", not "unlicensed"
and not "GPL-2.0".** That is why the index says `NOASSERTION`: there is no SPDX identifier for it.

### 🔴 Measured nil — recorded because silence looks exactly like coverage

🔴 **There is no deployable, LMS-integrated open-source accessibility platform.**
`course accessibility checker lms` returns **`1`** repository
([`johnnyrobot/canvas-accessible-content`](https://github.com/johnnyrobot/canvas-accessible-content),
Apache-2.0, **`0★`**). The category that institutions actually buy — a checker wired into Canvas or
Moodle that scores a course and nags the instructor — is **owned outright by the commercial layer**
(Anthology Ally and its peers). 🟢 **This is the clearest build-versus-buy gap in the education KB:**
every component needed to construct it is permissively licensed and on `repos/foundations.md`, and
the integrated product does not exist in open source. See `P246`.

### 🔴 Enumerated and NOT filed — with the reason

- [`brailleapps/dotify`](https://github.com/brailleapps/dotify) — **`ARCHIVED`**, last pushed `2016-10-17`, `3★`, no grant indexed. Ten years dead. Recorded so the braille-formatting layer is not re-searched under this name.
- [`fluid-project/infusion`](https://github.com/fluid-project/infusion) — `140★`, index `NOASSERTION`, last pushed `2025-01-15`. The Inclusive Design Research Centre's UI toolkit; on-topic and historically important, **not filed** on staleness plus an unresolved grant.
- [`rhasspy/piper`](https://github.com/rhasspy/piper) — MIT, `11,322★`, but **`ARCHIVED`** and last pushed `2025-08-26`. 🔵 Worth flagging loudly: it is the obvious permissive TTS for a speech-output product, it is MIT, it has eleven thousand stars — **and it is archived**. A successor will find it and assume it is live. It is not.
- `daisy/pipeline-assembly` — `7★`, no grant at `5` paths; a build harness, not a deployable system.


## 🆕 `p748` — the money layer a client already runs: the deployable aid, fee and scholarship systems, with the licence posture drawn per region, `2026-11-10`

the ones with an **install base and a budget**: a bursar's office that cannot close a term is a
procurement emergency in a way that a tutoring pilot never is. 🔴 **And the headline is a
dependency, not a platform:** the single most credible system here — a province's production student
financial aid platform, Apache-2.0, shipping every sprint — pins **Camunda 8**, whose grant is
**non-production only** (`T411`). Copy the architecture; replace that component before the pilot
ships (`P243`).

### Measured nil — recorded because silence looks like coverage

🔴 **LATAM scholarship-administration supply is a near-nil, and it was searched in-language.**
`becas sistema gestion` returns `42` repositories and the **highest-starred is `1★`**; every result
on the first page is an unlicensed student project (`RaisingSoftV2/sgb` `1★` `2012`,
`eugeluz/becas-unt` `1★`, `Mapeflo/GestionBecas` `0★`). `bolsa de estudos sistema` returns `12`,
`mensalidade escolar sistema` returns `5`, `gestion matricula pagos universidad` returns **`1`**.
🟢 **So the LATAM path in this register is `sapos` (MIT, real, institution-proven) plus
`bcgov/SIMS` as the architectural reference, not a regional platform** — which is what `P244`
builds from.


## 🆕 `p747` — the deployable federation: the platform an NREN already runs, with the licence posture drawn per region, `2026-11-10`

**`5` rows · all `5` payloads byte-read · `1` filed **with a refusal flag** because its `LICENSE`
file is a pointer rather than a grant (`T399`) · `3` platforms enumerated and NOT filed.**

🔵 **What this shelf gains that `p736` did not give it.** `p736` filed the SSO *server*. A national
research-and-education network does not deploy a server, it deploys a **federation**: a metadata
registry, a discovery service, an entitlement workflow and a wireless-roaming configuration tool.
Those are deployable systems with real install bases, and all five rows below are in production at a
named NREN today.

### 🔴 `T399` — a file named `LICENSE` can be a pointer rather than a grant, and it bit on the most-starred row

[`GEANT/CAT`](https://github.com/GEANT/CAT) (`124★`, indexed `NOASSERTION`) has a `LICENSE` file of
`1,095 B`, digest `e772049431f9`. 🔴 **It is a C comment block, not a licence.** It records GÉANT's
copyright and EU framework-programme grant numbers (GN3 `238875`, GN3plus `605243`, GN4-1 `691567`,
GN4-2 `731122`) and then says:

> *"License: see the `web/copyright.inc.php` file in the file structure or `<base_url>/copyright.php`
> after deploying the software"*

🟢 **Resolved by a second fetch** (`web/copyright.inc.php`, `200` on the raw channel): the real terms
are a **bespoke English-law licence** — derivative works must be licensed under the same licence,
no endorsement, **and an explicit *"No patent licence is granted"*** beyond one narrow clause.
🔴 **Three consequences, and the third is the one that matters:** the index says nothing; the
root-filename probe says *"granted"*; and **the grant can only be read by deploying the software or
fetching an application template by hand.** Any gate that treats `LICENSE`-exists as granted scores
this permissive. ⚠️ **Not OSI-standard, copyleft-shaped, no patent grant — treat as refused for
build-on work** and integrate over its published interfaces instead.

| [CSCfi/rems](https://github.com/CSCfi/rems) | 68 | 🟢 **MIT** · `1,088 B` · `b6f626a5d50b` | **2026-11-10** | **EMEA** (CSC — IT Center for Science, Finland) | **Resource Entitlement Management System: the apply-and-approve workflow for access to sensitive data.** 🟢 **The most directly AI-relevant platform on this page** — before an agent reads student or research records, *something* has to record who approved that access and for how long. REMS is that something, it is MIT, and it is in national production |

## 🆕 `p746` — the national EMIS tier: the deployable system a ministry already runs, with the copyleft posture drawn per layer, `2026-11-10`

> **Pass p746 · every grant byte-read 2026-11-10.** This shelf exists for *"the Odoo for ERP,
> OpenMRS for healthcare"* class. 🟢 **This pass found the missing sibling of that very comparison:
> `DHIS2` is to education data what `OpenMRS` is to clinical records — same sector, same funders,
> often the same ministry — and it was absent from every live shelf on this base.** `5` platform
> rows, `1` of them a `P210` promotion that has been sitting in the trending logs unshelved.

🔵 **The posture question a buyer actually asks, answered once for the whole tier.** Three different
copyleft regimes appear here and they are **not** interchangeable:

| Platform | Grant (byte-read) | Can a studio host a modified derivative as a client service? |
|---|---|---|
| **DHIS2** | 🟢 `BSD-3-Clause` | 🟢 **Yes, with no reciprocal obligation.** The most permissive posture of any platform on this shelf at this size |
| **OpenEMIS** | 🟡 `GPL-2.0` | 🟡 **Yes — `GPL-2.0` has no network clause.** Verified by bytes: `0` occurrences of `Remote Network Interaction` or `Affero` in the payload (`T344`'s discriminator). Distributing a *modified binary* carries the obligation; **hosting it does not** |
| **Giga services** (`giga-meter`, `giga-maps`) | 🔴 `AGPL-3.0` | 🔴 **No, not without accepting §13.** Network copyleft reaches the hosted service itself |

### The `5` rows

| Platform | Repo | Stars | Forks | Licence (byte-read 2026-11-10) | Region | What it is, and the seam |
|---|---|--:|--:|---|---|---|
| **DHIS2** | [dhis2/dhis2-core](https://github.com/dhis2/dhis2-core) | **361** | **409** | 🟢 **BSD-3-Clause** · `1,517 B` · sha256₁₂ `4fc00af93f1b` · © *2004-2025, University of Oslo* | **EMEA** (Norway, from the payload holder) | **The platform row of the pass.** A national-scale aggregate + tracker + analytics system for student and staff information at school, district and national level, with an official **Education packages** configuration layer ([`dhis2-emis-docs`](https://github.com/dhis2/dhis2-emis-docs), `BSD-3-Clause`, `1,492 B`, `7a337e1b37bd`). 🟢 **Customisation is a supported extension point, not a fork:** [`dhis2/app-platform`](https://github.com/dhis2/app-platform) (`BSD-3-Clause`, `1,518 B`, `923e34a49433`) ships custom apps *into* DHIS2 with inverted app control, and [`dhis2/ui`](https://github.com/dhis2/ui) (`1,505 B`, `a7456f880530`) supplies the native design system. ⚠️ **Install-base claims are NOT carried here** — the figures circulating for DHIS2 country deployments are vendor- and foundation-published and `725-F` applies. **What this pass verified by instrument: `409` forks, `252` repositories in the org, and a push on `2026-11-10`** |
| **DHIS2 Android** | [dhis2/dhis2-android-capture-app](https://github.com/dhis2/dhis2-android-capture-app) | 65 | 142 | 🟢 **BSD-3-Clause** · `1,478 B` · sha256₁₂ `eee2d8a9e7f7` · © *2004-2019, University of Oslo* | **EMEA** (Norway, payload holder) | **Offline-first** field data capture — the half of an EMIS deployment that decides whether rural schools report at all. 🔵 Paired with [`dhis2/dhis2-android-sdk`](https://github.com/dhis2/dhis2-android-sdk) (`1,477 B`, `67a5e1d977d8`) when the client has to be bespoke rather than branded. ⚠️ **Offline-first is a licence-adjacent design fact, not a marketing one:** it is why the `brianmituka/dhis2-mcp` small-local-model row on [`../agents/top.md`](../agents/top.md) is filed at all |
| **DHIS2 dashboards** | [dhis2/dashboard-app](https://github.com/dhis2/dashboard-app) | 45 | 46 | 🟢 **BSD-3-Clause** · `1,519 B` · sha256₁₂ `3212fbc149ec` · © *2004-2017, University of Oslo* | **EMEA** (Norway, payload holder) | The reporting surface a ministry official actually opens. 🔵 Filed because it is the natural host for an AI-authored indicator narrative: **the agent writes into a dashboard the user already trusts**, rather than into a new tool nobody has adopted |
| **OpenEMIS** 🟢 *promotion* | [OpenEMIS/core](https://github.com/OpenEMIS/core) | 24 | 20 | 🟡 **GPL-2.0** · `LICENSE` · `15,518 B` · sha256₁₂ `b6f03c6715ee` · stock FSF preamble · *index agrees* · 🟢 **`0` occurrences of `Remote Network Interaction` / `Affero` — no network clause** | **Global** (the OpenEMIS programme is a UNESCO-associated EMIS deployed through national ministries; **no single-country placement is asserted**) | Student and staff information management at school, district and national level — **the direct functional alternative to a DHIS2 education deployment.** 🟢 **`P210`-class promotion and a long-overdue one: this base carries `241` `OpenEMIS` mentions and an MCP bridge for it in the trending logs, and has never given the platform itself a row on this shelf.** 🔴 **The licence posture is the reason to read the cell: `GPL-2.0` is often reflexively treated as disqualifying for a hosted deliverable, and the bytes say it is not** — the reciprocal duty attaches to *distributing a modified version*, and this payload carries no network clause. ⚠️ Pushed `2026-11-04`; [`OpenEMIS/core-docker`](https://github.com/OpenEMIS/core-docker) (`7★`) carries **no grant** and was last pushed 2020 — deploy from source, not from that image |
| **Giga Meter** | [unicef/project-connect-daily-check-app](https://github.com/unicef/project-connect-daily-check-app) | 9 | 9 | 🔴 **AGPL-3.0** · `LICENSE` · `34,693 B` · sha256₁₂ `b25b37948bd2` — 🔵 **byte-identical to `unicef/giga-meter-backend`**, so the client and its backend ship one grant, not two | **Global** (UNICEF–ITU) | Windows desktop application that **automatically measures a school's real connectivity** and reports it — the ground-truth feed behind Giga's school-connectivity map. 🔴 **Read the licence cell before the description: this is the AGPL half of `T389`'s seam.** 🟢 **Deployable as-is to a ministry at no licence cost**; 🔴 **not forkable into a branded client service** without accepting §13. ⚠️ Pushed `2026-11-10` — the most actively maintained Giga artefact |

### 🔴 Enumerated and NOT filed — with the reason

- 🔴 **[`dhis2-metadata/EMIS_AGG`](https://github.com/dhis2-metadata/EMIS_AGG), the actual education metadata package, carries no grant** at `6` probed paths (index agrees: `NONE`). ⚠️ **So the deployable engine is `BSD-3-Clause` and the education configuration on top of it is ungranted** — a `P742-B`-shaped split. **Terms for the package must come from the implementing ministry; the engine is not in question.** Recorded as a `T302` honest negative, **not** a compliant absence, because the denominator channel is closed this session (`T388`).
- ⚠️ **`org:dhis2-metadata` is health-dominated and should not be re-swept for education:** `85` packages, of which the education one is the `0★` row above. The rest are malaria, immunisation-safety, neglected tropical diseases and integrated disease surveillance. **Measured, so no successor pays for it twice.**
- ⚠️ **`OpenEduCat`** surfaced on the mandated vertical-platform leg as an Odoo-based education ERP, **and the leg's own sources did not state its licence.** 🔴 **Not filed on that basis** — an unread grant is not a row. Named here as the standing candidate it is, for a pass that will byte-read it.

Licence is the decisive field here, because an LMS is almost always *offered as a service* —
which is exactly what the AGPL network clause is written about. Read the posture column
before proposing a fork.


## 🆕 `p745` — the lockdown-and-proctoring estate a university already runs, and the `149★` platform the search index files as ungranted, `2026-11-10`

> 🟢 **`5` rows, every grant byte-read today, `1` index value corrected from the bytes — and that
> one correction is the row of the pass.** This is the estate that sits between the LMS and the
> candidate at the moment an exam is taken: the layer the EU AI Act's Service Desk uses as its
> *worked* Annex III example, and therefore the layer a studio cannot subcontract to a vendor
> without taking on the vendor's classification.

### 🔴 `T382` — Safe Exam Browser for macOS and iOS is MPL-2.0, and nothing in the search index can tell you so

[`SafeExamBrowser/seb-mac`](https://github.com/SafeExamBrowser/seb-mac) is **`149★`, `69` forks,
pushed `2026-11-09`**, and the GitHub index reports its licence as **`NOASSERTION`**. It is
**MPL-2.0**. The grant is at **`Resources/LICENCE`** — and that single path trips *two* of the
shared probe's three documented traps simultaneously:

- **Trap #2, the licence lives in a subdirectory.** There is no root payload. A root-only ladder
  returns nothing and writes the row down as ungranted.
- **Trap #3, British spelling.** `LICENCE`, not `LICENSE`. `p14` priced the full case-and-spelling
  ladder at `0` payouts in `98` repositories and `p15` re-bought `LICENCE` alone on one payout in
  `44`. **This is that one request paying for itself at `149★`.**

🔴 **And the trap inside the trap:** the repository *does* contain two files named exactly
`LICENSE`, at `Pods/JitsiMeetSDK/LICENSE` (Apache-2.0, `558 B`) and
`SEB/QRCodeReaderViewController/LICENSE` (MIT, `1,089 B`). **Both are vendored third parties.** A
probe that searched the tree for `LICENSE` and stopped at the first hit would not have reported
"ungranted" — it would have reported **Apache-2.0 or MIT for a Mozilla-licensed application**,
which is a *worse* failure than the index's own abstention, and the one a hand-rolled sweep makes.

🟢 **The three MPL-2.0 payloads in this estate also settle the sizing question for good** (`T383`):
`seb-win-refactoring` is `16,726 B` / `fab3dd6bdab2`, `seb-mac` is `16,726 B` / `3f3d9e0024b1` —
**identical byte count, different bytes** — and `seb-screen-proctoring-server` is `16,725 B`.
Three files, one publisher, one licence, and **size identifies none of them**.

### The rows

| Platform | Repo | Stars | Forks | Licence (byte-read 2026-11-10) | Region | What it is, and the posture to take |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| **Safe Exam Browser (macOS / iOS)** | [SafeExamBrowser/seb-mac](https://github.com/SafeExamBrowser/seb-mac) | 149 | 69 | 🔴 **MPL-2.0** · `Resources/LICENCE` · `16,726 B` · sha256₁₂ `3f3d9e0024b1` — 🔴 **index says `NOASSERTION`; CORRECTED from the bytes** | **EMEA** (Switzerland — `Copyright (c) 2010-2026 Daniel R. Schneider, ETH Zurich, IT Services` and `Copyright 2010 ETH Zurich LET`, from the source headers) | The Apple-platform half of the most widely deployed open lockdown browser in higher education. 🟢 **MPL-2.0 is the posture that makes this estate usable:** copyleft is **per-file**, so a studio's own modules stay its own and only edits to SEB's files travel back. That is a materially different conversation from TAO's GPL-2.0 or Moodle's GPL-3.0, and it is why `P227` can exist at all |
| **Safe Exam Browser (Windows)** | [SafeExamBrowser/seb-win-refactoring](https://github.com/SafeExamBrowser/seb-win-refactoring) | 368 | 228 | 🟢 **MPL-2.0** · `LICENSE.txt` · `16,726 B` · `fab3dd6bdab2` · *index agrees* | **EMEA** (Switzerland — ETH Zürich) | 🟢 **A `P210` backlog promotion, not a discovery.** It has carried `evidence/address-ledger.tsv` and trending-log rows for several passes and **was never promoted to a shelf** — the exact defect `P210` names, found here only because `seb-mac` forced a look at the estate. The highest-starred row in this tier |
| **SEB Server** | [SafeExamBrowser/seb-server](https://github.com/SafeExamBrowser/seb-server) | 97 | — | 🟢 **MPL-2.0** · *already shelved; re-confirmed this pass, not re-filed* | **EMEA** (Switzerland) | Central configuration and exam administration for SEB clients — the piece that makes the estate an institutional deployment rather than a download |
| **SEB Screen Proctoring** | [SafeExamBrowser/seb-screen-proctoring-server](https://github.com/SafeExamBrowser/seb-screen-proctoring-server) | 7 | 2 | 🟢 **MPL-2.0** · `LICENSE` · `16,725 B` · `1f256ecad192` · © *2024 ETH Zürich, IT Services* *(holder in the source headers)* · *index agrees* | **EMEA** (Switzerland) | 🔴 **Self-described "proof of concept", pushed `2026-11-10` — today.** Filed because of *what it is*, not what it is ready for: **an open, self-hosted screen-proctoring service**, which is the one component every institution currently rents from a US vendor and the one the Service Desk uses as its Annex III worked example. 🔵 **Watch this repository.** If ETH ships it, the build-vs-buy answer for European proctoring changes, and `P227` is the recipe already written for that day |
| **UNIwise Originality for Moodle** | [UNIwise/moodle-plagiarism_uniwise](https://github.com/UNIwise/moodle-plagiarism_uniwise) | 1 | 0 | 🔴 **GPL-3.0** · `COPYING.txt` · **`35,147 B`** · `8ceb4b9ee5ad` — a **third** GPL-3.0 byte length here (`35,141` / `35,149` already held) · holder `copyright 2026 UNIwise` in `version.php` | **EMEA** (Denmark — basis: the UNIwise publishing record, stated as org provenance rather than a holder jurisdiction) | The WISEflow originality plugin. 🔵 **Filed for the rule it proves, not for `1★`:** the same company's infrastructure repositories are Apache-2.0 and MIT, and this one is GPL-3.0 **because Moodle's plugin API is**. The integration point sets the licence. A studio that plans a Moodle plugin has already chosen GPL-3.0, whatever its own policy says |

### 🔵 One non-software row, filed because the tier's market map is a repository

[`privateai0/proctoring-compatibility-2026`](https://github.com/privateai0/proctoring-compatibility-2026)
ships **no grant across its `1`-entry tree** and is therefore unusable as content. But what it
*contains* is a current compatibility matrix across **LockDown Browser, Proctorio, ProctorU and
Honorlock** — the four incumbents a Globant engagement will be measured against. It is cited in
`intel/market.md` as a competitor list, with its licence status stated, and nothing is copied from it.

## 🆕 `p744` — the library-services estate a university already runs, with the licence posture drawn per layer, `2026-11-10`

> 🟢 **`9` rows, every grant byte-read today, `3` index values corrected from the bytes.** This is
> the tier `p743` predicted but did not reach: not the research-data platform (Invenio,
> Dataverse) and not the LMS, but the **library services platform, the catalogue and the
> discovery layer** — the systems through which a university's students and faculty actually
> reach content.
>
> 🔵 **Read the posture column, because this shelf's licence spread is the widest on this base
> and it is not random.** The *catalogues* are copyleft (Koha `GPL-3.0-or-later`, VuFind
> `GPL-2.0`, Omeka S `GPL-3.0`, CKAN `AGPL-3.0-or-later`, Janeway `AGPL-3.0`, EPrints
> `LGPL-3.0`); the *platform frameworks and the discovery layer* are permissive (FOLIO
> `Apache-2.0`, Blacklight `Apache-2.0`, ArchivesSpace `ECL-2.0`). 🟢 **`T360`'s rule — decide
> "can we build on this?" by layer, not by repository — holds on `9 / 9` rows here**, and it is
> the reason `P220` puts the permissive discovery layer in front of the copyleft catalogue rather
> than forking the catalogue.
>
> 🔴 **`AGPL-3.0` is the decisive cell on this page and two rows carry it.** An LMS or a catalogue
> is almost always *offered as a service*, which is exactly what the network clause is written
> about. `ckan/ckan` indexes `NOASSERTION` and would read as unencumbered to anyone who trusts
> the index.

### 🔴 The mandated vertical leg nil'd again — the `38`th consecutive time

`open source education platform {industry} ERP CRM MIT Apache` returned OpenEduCat, Canvas, Sakai,
ILIAS, Chamilo, OpenOlat and LearnHouse. **Every one is already held.** It also asserted that
*"Sakai … has roots in a 2004 university consortium that included MIT"* in a result set about
**licences** — a sentence a careless pass reads as `MIT` — and published Open edX's licence in two
mutually contradictory forms in the same result set. 🔵 **This base already holds Sakai correctly
as `ECL-2.0` and Open edX correctly as `AGPL-3.0`.** All `9` rows above came from `org:` legs on
the index channel; the mandated prose leg produced `0`.

> and binary forms` with a `Neither the name of` clause → **BSD-3-Clause**, holder
> **`Copyright (c) Ministerium für Schule und Bildung des Landes NRW`**.
>
> 🔴 **And GitHub's index says `NOASSERTION`.** A pass that trusted the index would have skipped
> the single most procurement-credible platform row this base has added in weeks. This is `T338`
> and it is paired with its exact inverse at `T341`, where the index asserts `GPL-3.0` and
> **`19` probed paths return `404`**. Neither direction is safe: **the index is a hint, the bytes
> are the verdict.**
>
> 🔵 **Why a state's own SIS is worth more than its star count.** `SVWS` is the successor to
> `SchILD-NRW`, the administration software of North Rhine-Westphalia's schools — roughly
> `2.5 M` pupils' worth of institutional estate. `42` repositories, pushed today, with the
> statistics interface to `IT.NRW` tracked in the open (`svws-statkue-diff`). For an EMEA public-
> sector engagement, *"we extended the ministry's own BSD-licensed server"* is a different
> conversation from *"we propose a new platform"* — and BSD-3-Clause, unlike the `AGPL-3.0` of
> `HPI Schul-Cloud` on this page, lets a studio keep a proprietary layer and host it.

## The classroom-conferencing estate, a German sovereign school cloud, and three copyleft content platforms 🆕 new section at `p738`

**`6` rows, every licence read from the bytes today.** 🟡 **The honest summary of this pass's
platform shelf: the deployable systems are real, mature and almost all copyleft — and the
*gradient* within copyleft is the finding, because it decides what a studio may actually ship.**
`p738` read **LGPL-3.0**, **GPL-3.0** and **AGPL-3.0** platforms in the same sweep, and they are
three materially different commercial positions that this shelf has previously lumped together.

| Platform | Repo | Stars | Licence (gate · file · raw bytes) | Last push | Region | Posture — what you may and may not do |
|---|--:|---|---|---|---|---|
| **BigBlueButton** (Greenlight front end) | [bigbluebutton/greenlight](https://github.com/bigbluebutton/greenlight) | 850 | **LGPL-3.0** · `LICENSE` · `7,651 B` | **2026-11-09** | Global | 🟢 **The open-source virtual-classroom platform, and the one every LMS already integrates with.** Not a generic meeting tool: multi-user whiteboard, breakout rooms, polling, shared notes, a presenter model built for teaching, and **session recording with playback** (`bbb-playback`). The estate is `79` repositories and is pushed daily. 🟢 **Posture — and this is why it is filed above the AGPL rows: LGPL-3.0 is the mildest copyleft on this shelf.** Its reciprocity reaches **modifications to BigBlueButton itself**, and it explicitly contemplates being *linked to* by separately licensed software — so a studio can build and keep a proprietary layer *around* it and must publish only what it changes *inside* it. AGPL would not permit that for a hosted service. 🔵 **The AI entry points are first-party and already there**: `bbb-lti-broker` (LGPL-3.0, `7,652 B`) mounts it inside Moodle, Canvas or Open edX over LTI, `bbb-transcription-controller` is the live-captioning hook, and `bigbluebutton-html-plugin-sdk` is the documented surface for adding panels to the live classroom. ⚠️ `bbb-transcription-controller` and the plugin SDK carry **no grant at the probed paths** — the hooks you most want are the ones whose licence is unstated, so read before depending. ⚠️ Heavy to self-host (media servers, TURN, recording workers). |
| **HPI Schul-Cloud** | [hpi-schul-cloud/schulcloud-client](https://github.com/hpi-schul-cloud/schulcloud-client) | 34 | **AGPL-3.0** · `LICENSE` · `34,520 B` | **2026-11-10** | **EMEA** | 🟡 **Germany's state-funded school cloud, as a live codebase rather than a policy document** — the Hasso Plattner Institute lineage behind the `dBildungscloud` / Niedersachsen and Thüringen deployments. `131` repositories, pushed today: `nuxt-client` (the current front end), `schulcloud-calendar`, a **tldraw-based collaborative whiteboard client**, `antivirus_check_service`, and `dof_app_deploy` — the **actual Kubernetes deployment** of a national school platform, in the open. 🔴 **Posture: AGPL-3.0, so Globant cannot host a derivative as a product** without releasing the derived source. Do not fork it for a client product. 🟢 **Read it, and sell against it, for two concrete reasons.** First, it is the **reference answer to "what does a GDPR-and-AI-Act-compliant school platform actually look like in production"** — a question every EMEA public-sector bid asks and almost nobody can answer with running code (`T321`, `P196`). Second, where a German state *already runs it*, the engagement is **integration and AI layering over an existing estate**, which is licence-clean over LTI (`P186`) and is a larger and safer piece of work than a replacement. ⚠️ German-language-first; heavily coupled to German school-administration concepts. |
| **CrypTool 2** | [CrypToolProject/CrypTool-2](https://github.com/CrypToolProject/CrypTool-2) | 169 | **Apache-2.0** · `LICENSE` · `11,357 B` | 2026-11-02 | **EMEA** | 🟢 **The only Apache-2.0 deployable learning platform this pass found, and it is EMEA-authored and still maintained after two decades.** A visual-programming e-learning environment for cryptography and cryptanalysis from the German CrypTool project (university-led, Kassel/Siegen lineage): learners assemble ciphers, attacks and analyses as data-flow workflows and watch them run. 🟢 **Filed because it is a working, permissively licensed answer to the hardest ask in technical education — "teach a subject by letting the learner operate it"** — and because its shape generalises far past cryptography: the workflow canvas plus a library of inspectable components is exactly what a security, data-engineering or networking curriculum needs, and Apache-2.0 means a studio can take it there. 🔵 Pairs with the held sandbox pair (`openHPI/codeocean` + `poseidon`, `737-A`) for the half of such a curriculum that needs to run arbitrary learner code. ⚠️ **.NET/WPF, Windows-first** — the real constraint, and it is a serious one for a browser-delivered engagement. ⚠️ Subject-specialised content; the platform generalises, the component library does not. |
| **Kiwix** | [kiwix/kiwix-js](https://github.com/kiwix/kiwix-js) | 495 | **GPL-3.0** · `LICENSE-GPLv3.txt` · `35,147 B` | **2026-11-07** | Global | 🟢 **Offline content delivery, which is the unglamorous precondition for most of this base's LATAM, African and rural-APAC opportunity.** Kiwix serves whole compressed libraries — Wikipedia, Wiktionary, Project Gutenberg, Stack Exchange, and `.zim` archives you build yourself — with **no connectivity at all**, across browser, desktop (`1,545★`), Android (`1,534★`) and iOS (`815★`). The estate is pushed daily. 🔴 **Posture: GPL-3.0 — reciprocal on distribution, but *not* on hosting**, so the network clause that blocks AGPL platforms does not apply here; a studio that ships an unmodified Kiwix alongside its own separately licensed content has no source-release duty, and a studio that *modifies* Kiwix and distributes it does. 🟢 **The AI pattern is RAG without a network**: a `.zim` library plus a local model gives a tutor that works in a school with no internet, and the corpus is auditable because it is a file. 🔵 **Its licence file is also this pass's `738-E` control** — the grant lives at `LICENSE-GPLv3.txt`, which the old six-name chain could not see. ⚠️ `.zim` authoring is its own toolchain. |
| **Pressbooks** | [pressbooks/pressbooks-book](https://github.com/pressbooks/pressbooks-book) | 21 | **GPL-3.0** · `LICENSE.md` · `35,147 B` | **2026-11-10** | North America | 🟡 **The open-textbook publishing platform university libraries actually run** — WordPress-multisite-based authoring and multi-format export (web, EPUB, PDF) for OER, with `53` repositories pushed today, an **LTI provider**, **SAML and CAS single sign-on** (both pushed today), MathJax rendering and a network catalogue. 🟢 Filed because the **OER production pipeline** is where AI has an unarguable role — generating ancillaries, alternative explanations, question banks and translations for an open textbook the institution already owns — and this is the system that holds those textbooks. 🔴 **Posture: GPL-3.0, WordPress-plugin ecosystem**, so your extensions are plugins and are themselves GPL; distribution-reciprocal, not hosting-reciprocal. 🔵 The SSO and LTI plugins mean it slots into an existing campus estate rather than replacing anything. ⚠️ WordPress operational burden; the whole estate inherits it. |
| **Clusive** | [cast-org/clusive](https://github.com/cast-org/clusive) | 16 | ⚠️ **BSD-3-Clause, on the README's statement only** · no licence file at `14` probed paths · `package.json` says `ISC` | 2026-04-22 | North America | 🟢 **CAST's adaptive reading-and-learning environment — Universal Design for Learning implemented by UDL's own authors**, with learner-configurable text presentation, vocabulary and comprehension supports, reflection prompts and a teacher dashboard. Ships with **over 300 public-domain and openly licensed EPUBs**. 🟢 Filed because UDL is named in US state guidance and in accessibility procurement language, and because *adaptive reading support* is a far more defensible AI claim than *AI tutor*: the model reshapes presentation and scaffolding, not the curriculum. 🔴 **Read `738-F` before you depend on this.** There is **no licence payload**: the README says BSD-3-Clause, the `package.json` says `ISC` (which is `npm init`'s default value), and the GitHub index says `NOASSERTION`. The README is the most credible of the three and BSD-3-Clause would be excellent — **but get it in writing from CAST before a client build**, because a README sentence is not a licence file. ⚠️ Django + Python, `16★`, last pushed April 2026. |

### 🟢 `738-G` — the copyleft gradient, written down once so this shelf stops flattening it

This shelf has for many passes sorted platforms into *permissive* and *copyleft*. `p738` read all
three copyleft families in one sweep and the distinction inside that second bucket is the one that
actually decides engagements:

| Licence | Reciprocity triggers on | Can Globant host a modified version as a service? | Read on this shelf at `p738` |
|---|---|---|---|
| **LGPL-3.0** | modifying the library itself; linking is contemplated and permitted | 🟢 **Yes** — publish only your changes *inside* it | BigBlueButton (`7,651 B`) |
| **GPL-3.0** | **distributing** a derivative | 🟢 **Yes** — hosting is not distribution | Kiwix (`35,147 B`), Pressbooks (`35,147 B`) |
| **AGPL-3.0** | **offering it over a network**, modified | 🔴 **No** — not without releasing the derived source | HPI Schul-Cloud (`34,520 B`) |

🔵 **Why this is worth a table rather than a sentence:** the single most common way these
engagements go wrong is treating "copyleft" as one verdict and refusing a GPL platform that is in
fact deployable as a hosted service, or accepting an AGPL platform that is not. 🔴 **And it is
precisely the distinction `p736`'s gate defect destroyed** — a whole-payload match called every
GPL-3.0 repository AGPL-3.0 (`T311`), which would have moved Kiwix and Pressbooks from the green
row to the red one. The gate repair is load-bearing commercially, not just tidily.

### 🔴 `738-H` — the LATAM institutional-publisher channel is empty, measured on `14` legs

🔴 **The leg type that produced every fileable row in this pass and in `p737` produces *nothing*
in LATAM.** `p738` ran `14` publisher legs at Latin American ministries, agencies and
universities. The result:

| Leg | `total_count` | Fileable |
|---|--:|---|
| `org:SEDUC-SP`, `org:mec-br`, `org:inep`, `org:mineduc-cl`, `org:educacionar`, `org:uniandes`, `org:fundacion-telefonica`, `org:lappis-unb` | **no such organisation** | 0 |
| `org:pucp` | 0 | 0 |
| `org:AGESIC` | 1 (an e-signature utility) | 0 |
| `org:RNP` | 2 (both inactive since 2014) | 0 |
| `org:unam` | 2 | 0 |
| `org:USPCodeLab` | 15 (a student club: boilerplates, hackathon sites) | 0 |
| `org:govbr` | 17 (portal furniture and a mail CLI; nothing educational) | 0 |

🟢 **Read this together with `T323` and the conclusion is firm rather than gloomy: LATAM's
education institutions are not absent from the sector, they are absent from GitHub.** `T323`
already refuted the discovery-language explanation on `10` non-English legs. `p738` refutes the
remaining explanation — *"we are querying the wrong publishers"* — by querying the obvious
publishers by name and finding that **most of those organisations do not exist on GitHub at all.**
🔵 **Which relocates the LATAM opportunity precisely, and makes it bigger rather than smaller:**
the region's institutional capability is **not published as reusable software**, so a LATAM
engagement is not "integrate with what the ministry already open-sourced" (there is nothing) but
"build the institutional artefact, and the governance around it, from the regional components that
*do* exist" — the held `Plan Ceibal` / `Fundación Ceibal` estate, `LATAM-GPT`, `portabilis`,
`bncc-dev`, and the Iberian-language rows on the agent shelf. 🔵 It also explains why LATAM reads
thin on a GitHub-sourced base while reading **first in the world on adoption** in survey data
(`T333`). Denominators in `evidence/leg-ledger.2026-11-10-p738.tsv`.
## Sovereign national education services, and an MIT classroom-conferencing platform 🆕 new section at `p737`

**`4` rows, all read `2026-11-10`, all live, all from European publishers this base had never
addressed, `4 / 4` licence verdicts from the payload.** 🟢 **The headline is a licence fact with
immediate commercial consequence: the classroom video platform this shelf has always answered with
BigBlueButton has an actively-maintained MIT alternative at `1,350★`, pushed the day it was read.**
🟢 **The other three rows are *national public services* rather than products — France's statutory
digital-skills certification and Germany's largest free learning platform — which is a shelf
category this base did not have and which is exactly what a European public-sector engagement is
compared against.**

| Platform | Repo | Stars | Licence | Last push | Region | What it is, and the posture |
|---|---|--:|---|---|---|---|
| **eduMEET** | [edumeet/edumeet](https://github.com/edumeet/edumeet) | 1,350 | **MIT** | **2026-11-09** | **EMEA** | **Multiparty web conferencing built for education**, on mediasoup and WebRTC: rooms, lobbies, moderator roles, screen sharing, breakout rooms and chat, with a separate room server and recorder, designed to be run by an institution on its own infrastructure and to federate with research-network identity. It comes out of the European research-and-education networking community (GÉANT/NORDUnet lineage). Gate: `LICENSE.md`, `1,075 B` raw, `Permission is hereby granted, free of charge` → **MIT**; index agrees. 🟢 **Posture, and it is the reason this row matters more than its star count: this is the first *permissive* classroom-conferencing platform on this shelf.** BigBlueButton — the answer this base has given every time — is **LGPL-3.0 with an AGPL-3.0 HTML5 client**, so a studio hosting a modified version for a client inherits obligations it usually does not want. **eduMEET is MIT: fork it, brand it, embed it in a product, host it as a service, no reciprocal obligation.** 🔵 **Where that changes a proposal:** "AI in the live classroom" — transcription, real-time translation, participation analytics, an agent that attends and summarises — requires modifying the conferencing client, which is precisely the act the AGPL reaches. Under MIT it is a product decision instead of a licence decision (`P195`). 🔵 Pushed the day it was read; `edumeet-docker` (`68★`, MIT, `LICENSE`, `1,075 B`) is the maintained deployment path. ⚠️ **Honest comparison: BigBlueButton is the more complete *teaching* platform** — whiteboard, polling, breakouts and LMS integrations are deeper, and its Moodle and Canvas plugins are mature. eduMEET buys you licence freedom and self-hosting, not feature parity. ⚠️ mediasoup means real SFU operations (TURN, bandwidth, scaling) — not a managed service. 🟢 Region placed on the publisher's documented European research-network lineage. |
| **Pix** | [1024pix/pix](https://github.com/1024pix/pix) | 286 | **AGPL-3.0** ⚠️ | **2026-11-09** | **EMEA** | **France's public service for assessing and certifying digital skills** — adaptive position tests across sixteen competence areas, a scored certification with legal standing in French education and employment, and institutional dashboards for schools, universities and employers. It is a *national statutory service*, used across French secondary and higher education, and its code is published in the open. Gate: `LICENSE.md`, `34,280 B` raw, family line `GNU AFFERO GENERAL PUBLIC LICENSE` → **AGPL-3.0**; index agrees. 🔴 **Posture first, because it decides everything: AGPL-3.0's network clause is triggered by offering the software as a service, which is how a studio would deploy an assessment platform. Globant cannot build a hosted product on this.** 🟢 **It is filed anyway, and deliberately, for three uses the licence does permit.** 🔵 **One — it is the reference specification for adaptive certification.** When a ministry or a sector body asks for "a national skills certification like Pix", this *is* Pix: the competence framework, the adaptive item-selection logic, the scoring and the certification workflow, readable in full. Architecture is not licensed. 🔵 **Two — the component layer is MIT.** `1024pix/pix-ui` carries a separate MIT grant (on [`../repos/foundations.md`](../repos/foundations.md)), so the accessible assessment front end *can* be reused commercially while the AGPL core is not (`T327`). 🔵 **Three — an institution deploying it for itself triggers no obligation**, which is the clean route for a public-sector client who wants to run it rather than buy it. ⚠️ Large monorepo (API, several front ends, admin, LCMS), French-primary documentation, and the competence framework is tied to the French and EU DigComp reference. 🟢 Region placed on a named French public operator. |
| **Serlo** | [serlo/frontend](https://github.com/serlo/frontend) | 40 | **Apache-2.0** | 2026-10-31 | **EMEA** | **The application serving serlo.org — Germany's largest free, non-profit, openly-licensed learning platform**, covering school mathematics, the sciences and more as a structured taxonomy of articles, courses, exercises and worked solutions, with its own content editor. Gate: `LICENSE`, `11,357 B` raw, title line `Apache License` → **Apache-2.0**; index agrees. 🟢 **Posture, and it is the best on this entire shelf: Apache-2.0 across the whole estate — frontend, API and database layer alike.** A studio may fork it, host it, modify it and ship it commercially, with a patent grant. **No other national-scale learning platform this base holds is permissive**: Moodle is GPL-3.0, Open edX AGPL-3.0, Canvas AGPL-3.0, ILIAS GPL-3.0, Chamilo GPL-3.0. 🟢 **Why that is the finding rather than a detail:** every "can we build a product on an open LMS" conversation in this base has ended in a copyleft caveat. This one does not, and it comes with a real install base and a maintained content model. 🔵 **The AI composition is direct and unbuilt:** the platform already holds curriculum-aligned, openly-licensed German content in a typed taxonomy with a public GraphQL API — the retrieval corpus a grounded tutor needs — and the Apache-2.0 grant means the tutor can ship as a product (`P193`). ⚠️ Confirm the **content** licence per item separately: the code is Apache-2.0, the material is CC-licensed. ⚠️ `40★` and non-profit-scale engineering; it is a real platform but not a vendor product, and German-primary throughout. 🟢 Region placed on a named German non-profit publisher. |
| **Serlo ABC** | [serlo/serlo-abc](https://github.com/serlo/serlo-abc) | 31 | **Apache-2.0** | 2022-04-14 | **EMEA** | A **literacy-training application built for refugees** — first-language-independent alphabet and reading instruction, designed for low-literacy adult learners on a phone. Gate: `LICENSE`, `11,357 B` raw → **Apache-2.0**; index agrees. 🟡 **Filed with its staleness stated plainly: last pushed April 2022, the stalest row of this pass.** 🟢 **It is here because the *category* is one no other row in this base occupies, and because EMEA is where that category is funded.** Adult and migrant literacy is a standing, publicly-financed education obligation across Europe, the learner cannot read the interface by definition, and this is a permissively-licensed, purpose-built design for exactly that constraint. 🔵 **Read it for the interaction model rather than the code**: what an instructional UI looks like when text cannot carry the instructions is a hard design problem already solved here, and it transfers directly to any low-literacy or early-years AI tutor. ⚠️ **Four years without a push — treat it as a design reference, not a dependency**, and expect to rebuild the application around the model. 🟢 Region placed on a named German non-profit publisher. |

### 🟢 `737-C` — the permissive-platform gap this shelf has declared for many passes is closed, and it was closed by a non-profit

This shelf's `## Composition posture, by licence` section and `724-C` have recorded, repeatedly,
that the deployable-platform tier is **copyleft all the way down** — the reason every pattern in
this base reaches an LMS through LTI, MCP or a content type rather than by forking it.

🟢 **`p737` files the exception: `serlo/frontend`, `serlo/api.serlo.org` and `serlo/database-layer`
are all Apache-2.0, all from one publisher, and the platform they serve is a national-scale
learning site with openly licensed content.** The estate is permissive end to end, which no other
LMS-shaped thing on this shelf is.

⚠️ **The honest size of the claim.** Serlo is a **German non-profit at `40★`**, not Moodle at
`6,000★` with a plugin market and a certified-partner network. What closed is the *licence* gap, not
the *maturity* gap: a studio can now build and sell on an open national-scale learning platform,
and it will be doing the platform engineering itself. 🔵 **The two therefore compose rather than
compete** — propose Serlo when the client wants to *own a product*, and Moodle-plus-LTI when the
client wants to *extend what they already run* (`P186`).

🔵 **All four exist** — `README.md` returns `200` on the raw channel for each — so this is a grant
problem, not a dead-address problem. **Two independent channels agree** (GitHub's licence index is
`null`; `7` root filenames return `404`), which is the strongest verdict this environment permits.
⚠️ **It is still NOT an enumerated absence, and `p735` will not call it one.** `ACTION p275`
requires a tree enumeration for a negative grant verdict, and `735-B` establishes that the
enumeration channel is **unreachable here**: `api.github.com/repos/{owner}/{repo}` is `403`
unauthenticated, exactly as `T289` found for the authenticated client. **Recorded as
`no readable grant`, not as `grant-absent`.**

🟢 **Why this belongs on this page rather than in a footnote.** The single most-requested artefact in
an education engagement is a **student information / school-management system**, and a studio
cannot put an unlicensed repository into a client deliverable at any star count. `1,246★` of
apparent supply across these four resolves to **zero adoptable platforms** — which is why
`openeducat_erp` (LGPL-3.0), `Moodle` (GPL-3.0) and `SchoolOS-backend` (MIT) remain the real
shelf, and why `CoreLMS` above, at `62★`, outranks a `649★` repository for this purpose.
🔵 **The remedy is cheap and should be tried:** these are small, active projects whose authors can
simply be asked to add a licence file — the same remedy `p689` recorded for the two unlicensed
tutor benchmarks.

**`6` rows, all read `2026-11-10`, all live.** 🟢 **The first row is the most consequential
platform this page has taken since `p733`'s `XIAOJUSURVEY`, and for the opposite reason:** it is
not a new system but a **first-party hyperscaler deployment of the system clients already run**.
🔵 **Four of the six are EMEA**, and that is the first time this page has placed four European rows
in one pass — `T290` explains both why they appeared and why none of them is a learning agent.

| Platform | Repo | Stars | Licence | Last push | Region | What it is, and the posture |
|---|---|--:|---|---|---|---|
| **Modern Moodle on GCP** | [google/moodle-on-gcp](https://github.com/google/moodle-on-gcp) | 74 | Apache-2.0 | 2026-11-07 | Global | A **reference architecture and deployment kit for running Moodle on Google Cloud**, container- and Kubernetes-native, authored — per its own README — by *"Google Cloud's Engineers specialized in Education"*, with documentation covering architecture, deployment, load testing and cost. Gate: `LICENSE.md`, `11,357 B` raw → **`9942 B`/`3fda30957805`** (stock Apache; one of the `5` payloads establishing the constant). 🟢 **Why this matters more than `74★` suggests, and it is a licensing argument.** Moodle is **GPL-3.0** and the single most-deployed LMS in the world; this base's standing problem is that every Moodle proposal lands on a copyleft host. This repository is **Apache-2.0 infrastructure-as-code wrapping a GPL application** — the deployment, scaling and operations layer carries a permissive grant even though Moodle does not, because it is a *separate work that deploys* Moodle rather than a derivative of it. That is the cleanest available answer to "can we build on Moodle", and it comes from a first-party vendor with its name on it. 🔵 Pairs directly with `moodlehq/wiki-rag` (BSD-3-Clause, already held) for the AI layer. ⚠️ GCP-specific by construction; the architecture transfers, the Terraform does not. |

Gate: `LICENSE`, `1,068 B` raw → **`1020 B`/`fe2a9817987f`**. 🟢 **Filed for its licence above all else:** the course-delivery shelf on this page is **GPL-3.0 (Moodle), AGPL-3.0 (Open edX), AGPL-3.0 (Frappe LMS)** — and this is an **MIT** course platform at `110★`. Production systems **run by a named institution against its own enrolled students**, published
permissively. These are not products looking for adopters; they are systems with a live install
base of one, which is a different and often better reference for an engagement — the requirements
are already settled and visible in the code. All rows verified `2026-11-10`.

### Why Open edX's absent star count is recorded as absent

`search/repositories` has **no entry** for `openedx/edx-platform`: a direct query returns a
`422`, and `edx-platform in:name` returns only forks (`Edraak` 13★ archived,
`microsoftarchive` 5★ archived). Its `README.rst` and `LICENSE` both answer `200` on the raw
channel, and the `LICENSE` reads *GNU AFFERO GENERAL PUBLIC LICENSE Version 3* — read this
pass. So the project is alive and its licence is known; only the star field is unavailable.
A pass that treated index-absence as non-existence would delist the second-most-deployed
LMS in the industry. The cell is left marked rather than guessed.

That is the one assessment problem an LLM marker cannot be trusted with and a deterministic engine solves outright (`P161`'s objective oracle, available off the shelf). Exports **SCORM** and **QTI**, so it drops into an existing Moodle or Canvas gradebook. 🟢 **`Apache-2.0`, canonical body `9,478 B` / `399d47608b81`** — one of the `n=8` publishers establishing that constant at `729-C`, and the **only permissively-licensed examination platform on this shelf** (`TCExam` is `AGPL-3.0` plus a trademark restriction). Region **EMEA** (`newcastle-university` topic; Newcastle named in `README.md`). |


## Curriculum, courseware and district platforms 🆕 new section at `p729`

The `p728` section above is about *delivering an exam*. These four are about the three platform jobs
this shelf had no row for: **mapping a curriculum** across a multi-year programme, **running courseware**
whose lessons grade themselves, **serving an LMS as an API** for an agent to drive, and **standing up a
single district's credential programme**. Two of the four are also `729-B`'s exhibits — their stock-`MIT`
payloads fall outside the byte band this pass retired, in both cases because the copyright holder is an
institution.

## Not open source — do not stage these as starting points

These appear in open-source listings, in GitHub topic pages and in search results, and each one
fails on reading the licence file. 🔴 **Six rows were added at `p730` and they are the pass's
central finding (`730-A`/`T262`): of the twenty subjects verified, six of the seven largest by
stars land here.** For five of those six, `search/repositories` reported **`NOASSERTION`** — the
index said *unknown*, never *unusable*, and a pass that read the field instead of the payload would
have filed all of them.

| [weponusa/teachany](https://github.com/weponusa/teachany) | 296 | **custom dual licence** | "Instructional Design OS" for K-12. `LICENSE` is a bespoke dual licence (English/Chinese). Not adoptable without legal review. |

| [syauqi357/siakad-madrasah](https://github.com/syauqi357/siakad-madrasah) | 10 | **no licence at all** | Madrasah academic-information system (SvelteKit + REST), pushed `2026-07-09`. Same four-filename probe, same four `404`s. 🟢 New at p725. |

| [GarethManning/education-agent-skills](https://github.com/GarethManning/education-agent-skills) | 969 | 🟡 **CC BY-SA 4.0** — content, share-alike | `165` evidence-grounded agent skills for teachers, school leaders and edtech builders, across pedagogy, learning science, curriculum and assessment. 🟡 **Not a refusal on commerciality — a constraint on derivatives.** CC BY-SA permits commercial use with attribution, but **adaptations must be released under the same licence**, so a studio's edits to these skills cannot be kept proprietary. Usable as *reference pedagogy*; not stageable as a private deliverable. Index says `NOASSERTION`. 🟢 New at p730. |
| [canyongbs/advisingapp](https://github.com/canyongbs/advisingapp) | 340 | 🔴 **Elastic License 2.0** — not OSI | AI conversational assistant, student-success platform and recruitment CRM for colleges and universities (Canyon GBS). 🔴 **ELv2 forbids providing the software to third parties as a hosted or managed service**, which is the delivery model a studio engagement most often takes — and it is not an OSI-approved open-source licence, whatever the repository's visibility suggests. 🔵 **Worth reading anyway**: it is the clearest available specification of what a US higher-education student-success platform is expected to do. Index says `NOASSERTION`. 🟢 New at p730. |
| [learning-commons-org/knowledge-graph](https://github.com/learning-commons-org/knowledge-graph) | 167 | 🔴 **resolved per dataset, not per repository** | "The data layer for smarter educational AI" — trusted instructional content and research for grounding education tools. 🔴 **`LICENSE.md` does not grant anything: it explains that the licence and attribution are set *per dataset*, in that dataset's page in the project's own catalogue.** So there is no repository-level answer, and every dataset must be cleared individually before use. 🔵 Filed here rather than refused outright: **the repository is a licence-lookup index, and it should be used as one.** Index says `NOASSERTION`. 🟢 New at p730. |
| [bigdata-ustc/Agent4Edu](https://github.com/bigdata-ustc/Agent4Edu) | 102 | 🔴 **no grant at any ladder path** | LLM-agent generation of **learner response data** for intelligent education systems (AAAI 2025, oral). 🔵 Directly useful in principle — synthetic learner responses are what you need to test a tutor before real students touch it. 🔴 **`LICENSE`, `LICENSE.md`, `LICENSE.txt`, `LICENCE`, `COPYING` and `UNLICENSE` all return `404`; the index reports `NONE`.** No grant means no permission: readable, citable, **not usable**. 🟢 New at p730. |

## Composition posture, by licence

- **Permissive, customise freely:** Kolibri (MIT), **Mentingo (MIT)**, **MyClass (MIT, new at p724)**, **Digitren (MIT, new at p725 — but stale since 2025-11-22)**, **Skuul (MIT, new at p730 — multi-school, maintained)**, Sakai (ECL-2.0), Submitty (BSD-3), **CTFd (Apache-2.0, new at p730)**.
- 🆕 **MPL-2.0, the mildest copyleft on this page (new tier at p730):** **RS School App**, **Edrys**.
  File-level copyleft — publish your changes to *their* files, keep your own new files proprietary.
  This is the only tier that permits a closed module beside the platform, which makes it the
  easiest copyleft to propose for a studio deliverable. Both payloads read `16,725 B` /
  sha256₁₂ `1f256ecad192`, `0 B` spread at `n=2`.
- **GPL, safe to host:** Moodle, Chamilo, H5P. Distributing a modified build to the client
  requires source; running it for them does not.
- **AGPL, structure around it:** Open edX, Canvas, LearnHouse, **giua@school (new at p725)**. Build AI as **separate
  services talking over APIs/LTI**, not as core patches — this keeps the client's obligations
  unchanged and is also the cleaner upgrade path.
- **LGPL, middle ground:** BigBlueButton, OpenEduCat. Link against, don't fork.

### 🟡 `724-C` — the permissive education-ERP gap is re-measured and still open

The three stacks a client is most likely to already run were re-read today and **none of them moved**:
`frappe/lms` **`3,393★`, AGPL-3.0** (pushed `2026-11-09`) · `frappe/education` **`703★`, `NOASSERTION`**
(pushed `2026-10-30`) · `openeducat/openeducat_erp` **`908★`, LGPL-3.0** (pushed `2026-11-02`).
So the ERP/SIS half of this shelf is still **copyleft or licence-unclear at every scale that has an
install base**, and `p724`'s one MIT addition (`MyClass`, `110★`) sits two orders of magnitude below
them. 🔵 **The practical consequence is unchanged and worth restating:** for an institutional ERP/SIS
engagement the pattern is **AI as a separate service over APIs** (`P138`), not a fork — the permissive
greenfield option genuinely does not exist at institutional scale, and `P154` is only honest for
greenfield.

⚠️ **One candidate was probed and does not exist:** `openeducat/openeducat` returns a **`422`** from the
index (*"the listed users and repositories cannot be searched"*). The real address is
`openeducat/openeducat_erp`, already on this shelf. Recorded so a later pass does not re-derive it.

### 🟢 `723-M` — the permissive-LMS gap this shelf declared is now half-closed, and the repository was EMEA-authored

Every prior pass recorded the same structural problem: **every deployable LMS in education is
copyleft** (Moodle/Chamilo GPL-3.0; Open edX/Canvas/LearnHouse AGPL-3.0), leaving Kolibri — a
low-connectivity *content delivery* tool, not a full LMS — as the only permissive option, and
`Sakai` (ECL-2.0) as the only permissive LMS proper. `Selleo/mentingo` is an **MIT-licensed,
AI-native LMS, pushed today**, and it changes the default answer to *"can we fork the LMS?"*
from "not without a copyleft conversation" to "yes, on this one."

🔵 Two cautions, both load-bearing:

1. **It is small.** 109★ against Moodle's install base of ~200k sites. This is a
   *greenfield* option — a client with no LMS, or one building a workforce-learning product
   — **not** a migration target for an institution already running Moodle or Canvas.
2. **It is vendor-steered.** Selleo sells the same product as SaaS and as a custom build. The
   MIT grant on the published repository was read from the payload this pass and is real, but
   open-core projects re-licence; pin a commit and re-read `LICENSE` before each engagement
   (`P135`).

🔴 It also contradicts something this base has asserted for five consecutive passes —
that **"EMEA contributes almost nothing to open-source education supply"**. Selleo is a Polish
software house and Mentingo is platform-scale. 🔵 **And the sharpest part: this base filed
Mentingo at `p655`**, in the 128-row roster at the bottom of this same file, with its stack
read from the manifest. The EMEA supply nil was then re-asserted at p719, p720, p721 and p722
— four times — **with the counter-example sitting unread in the same file.** The nil was never
a fact about EMEA; it was a fact about which part of this file each pass looked at.
See [`../intel/trends.md`](../intel/trends.md) `T234`.

| **FenixEdu Academic** (university academic management) | [FenixEdu/fenixedu-academic](https://github.com/FenixEdu/fenixedu-academic) | 🟠 **LGPL-3.0** (`7,652 B`, `sha256₁₂ 97628afebc60`, payload read first-hand at p672 — `GNU LESSER GENERAL PUBLIC LICENSE, Version 3, 29 June 2007`) | **`master`** | Java / Spring, PostgreSQL | 🆕 **p672 — the first *EMEA-origin* system-of-record on this shelf, filed against the SIS layer `p668` measured end to end.** A full university academic-management system: degree and curricular-plan modelling, enrolment, class and space scheduling, marks and credits, student lifecycle. 🔵 **The adoption signal is the reason it is filed, and it is the strongest on this shelf by this base's own metric.** **`111` forks against `175` stars — forks-per-star `0.634`**, which puts it in the *deployed-per-institution* class alongside `i-educar` (`0.766`), the class `p540` separated from import-as-dependency. For a system of record a fork is an installation, so a ratio this high on a modest star count means **real institutions running modified copies** — the exact evidence the `p668` census found missing across most of this layer. 🟠 **Licence gate, stated plainly: LGPL-3.0 is weak copyleft.** Globant can build on it and keep proprietary code *separate*, but modifications **to FenixEdu itself** must ship back under LGPL — so the engagement shape is extension-and-integration, never a closed fork. That is a different scoping than the Apache-2.0 platforms on this page (`OpenOLAT`, `Artemis`). 🔴 **And the hard caveat: it is dormant. Pushed `2023-06-14` — roughly 3.4 years cold**, with `75` open issues. This is **not** a base to ship on its own authority; it is the one place on this shelf where a mature, genuinely-deployed European academic system can be read for its *domain model* (the curricular-plan and credit modelling is the hard part of any SIS) while the running code needs a maintenance assessment first. 🟡 **Placement is on this base's *weakest* channel and is flagged as such:** `search/users` returns `1` for `user:FenixEdu location:Portugal` **and** `location:Lisbon` — both spellings positive, per `667-G2` — but that is the **owner account's self-declared `location` field**, which `p668` ranked last; the repository's own README names no institution. **EMEA is established; the institution is not claimed.** |

| **ENT Core / Edifice** (French national *Espace Numérique de Travail*) | [`edificeio/entcore`](https://github.com/edificeio/entcore) | 🔴 **AGPL-3.0 — and `gh api` reports `NOASSERTION`, resolved here by reading the payload.** `LICENSE` at `master`, **`8,537 B`**, sha256₁₂ `86b7a74b9496`. It is **not** the canonical AGPL text but a **bespoke grant statement** — *“ENT Core is free software, licensed under the terms of the GNU Affero General Public License…”* — which is the named mechanism for the detector declining (`T99` class). The surrounding platform carries canonical AGPL-3.0 instead: [`edificeio/infra-front`](https://github.com/edificeio/infra-front) `34,520 B` sha256₁₂ `76a97c878c9c`, [`edificeio/springboard`](https://github.com/edificeio/springboard) `34,521 B` sha256₁₂ `432d251a78db` | `master` (pushed `2026-11-06`; liveness via `git ls-remote --symref`) | Java + Vert.x kernel, TypeScript front ends, PostgreSQL / MongoDB / Neo4j | 🆕 **Promoted at p695 from this base's own backlog, not discovered.** `edificeio/entcore` has been cited in [`../repos/trending.md`](../repos/trending.md) since `p406` — graded **canonical** against the `OPEN-ENT-NG` and `opendigitaleducation` mirrors — and had **never reached any of the three shelf tables**. This is `693-A`'s prescription executed a second pass running. It is the **modular kernel of the French state school platform**: the ENT is procured per *académie* / région, and each function ships as its own repository (attendance, competences, timetable, exercises, wiki, collaborative walls, room booking). 🔴 **Commercially the licence boundary is the whole story: every *education application* in the organisation is AGPL-3.0, and the kernel you must link against is the bespoke-grant AGPL above.** The permissive seam is real but sits one layer down — `17 / 74` canonical repositories are Apache-2.0 and **all of them are Vert.x infrastructure**, not education features (see the `p695` census below). 🟢 **So the sellable surface is integration and clients, per `T74`, never a closed derivative of the platform.** |
| **DSpace** | [DSpace/DSpace](https://github.com/DSpace/DSpace) | 🟢 **BSD-3-Clause** — `LICENSE`, **`1,504 B`**, sha256₁₂ `baf9ee18d437`, © *2002–2021 LYRASIS, all rights reserved*, read byte-for-byte at `main` on `2026-11-07`; `gh api` agrees (`spdx = BSD-3-Clause`) | `main` (`355dfa938a73`) | Java / Angular, REST API, OAI-PMH, SWORD, Solr | 🆕 **New at p696, and it is the most permissive large platform this shelf has ever gained — BSD-3 rather than the AGPL that governs most of this page.** DSpace is the dominant open-source **institutional repository**: theses, datasets, research output, with persistent identifiers and harvesting protocols, run by university libraries worldwide. 🔵 **The AI opportunity is specific and it is retrieval, not chat:** a DSpace instance is a university's own licensed, rights-cleared, metadata-rich corpus — the single best RAG substrate on a campus, and the BSD-3 grant means a studio can embed, index and ship derivatives without the copyleft question that stops an Open edX or Moodle derivative conversation. `1,108★`. Region **North America** (LYRASIS, US) by stewardship; deployment is global. |

Region **North America** (Apereo, US) by stewardship. |

The grant is unmodified; the tail is attribution for bundled dependencies | 🔴 `trunk` (`e103805ef7f1`) | Java, Groovy, Freemarker, entity engine, service engine | 🆕 **New at p696, and it resolves a licence contradiction this base's own ERP leg kept returning.** Secondary sources disagree on OFBiz's grant — one roundup read in this pass's mandated vertical leg asserts GPLv2 — and the **payload settles it: Apache-2.0**. A top-level ASF project and the only mature, genuinely permissive full ERP available to an education engagement, where the education-specific ERPs are copyleft (**OpenEduCat** LGPL-3.0, already on this shelf). 🔵 **The honest framing for a client:** OFBiz has **no education domain model** — it is a permissive general ERP spine onto which student, fee and enrolment entities must be modelled. An MIT CRM is the permissive base for an admissions-agent build: lead scoring, enquiry triage, applicant follow-up. 🟡 **It has no education data model** — applicant, programme, intake and cohort must be modelled on top; state that in the estimate. 🔴 **The default branch is `2.2`**: any probe assuming `main` reads this repository as missing. Those are design commitments with a named pedagogical framework behind them, not an accessibility checklist bolted on. 🟢 **Zero backend is the deployment finding:** no accounts, no database, no server, every bit of state in the browser — so it runs from a memory stick in a school with no connectivity and generates **no pupil data to protect**, which removes the entire LGPD/GDPR surface rather than mitigating it. The strongest offline row since `P52`, and `P84` builds on it.  |

|  **SchoolCircle**  |  [groundworklms/SchoolCircleLMS](https://github.com/groundworklms/SchoolCircleLMS)  |  🟢 **Apache-2.0** — `LICENSE`, **`11,340 B`**, sha256₁₂ `9fad4c07975e`; 🔵 **`18 B` short of canonical and the body is still `0`-byte verbatim** — `1 B` is a missing leading blank line and `17 B` is the **appendix filled in** (`Copyright [yyyy] [name of copyright owner]` → `Copyright 2026 Groundwork`)  |  `main` (pushed `2026-10-08`)  |  Orchestrates a platoon of standalone Apache-2.0 services; **Anchor** offline grounding engine; browser-deliverable  |  🆕 **New at p700 — and it is the clearest statement of the delivery guarantee this base has been assembling for a hundred passes: “Every answer cites the manual — or the system refuses.”** A **grounded, offline, human-led** learning platform. Every claim is routed through **Anchor**, an offline grounding engine that answers **only from cited source material or refuses**; AI drafts, **a human ratifies**, and *nothing unreviewed reaches a student*. 🟢 **That is `P72`, `T90` and `P60` implemented as architecture rather than as policy** — and the project names the guarantee as the product, which is exactly how it has to be sold into a regulated education buyer. 🟢 **The learner loop closes on calibration, not completion:** students are scored on confidence-versus-correctness, which is a measurable construct, unlike time-on-task. 🟢 **Offline by design**, so it is the only row on this shelf that satisfies `P52`/`P84`'s connectivity constraint *and* an Annex III-shaped audit trail at the same time. 🔵 **Filled Apache appendix is a bonus jurisdiction fact** — an unfilled appendix names nobody; this one names *Groundwork, 2026*, a third mechanism for `T127` in which the whole-file hash moves and the rights do not.  |

The learner app for **Euron Open LMS**: courses, a native classroom with **offline video**, quizzes and certificates. 🟢 **Offline video in the learner app is the differentiator** — Moodle and Open edX both reach mobile through a browser or a generic app; shipping the classroom as a native app with cached media is the shape that works on an intermittent connection, which is the majority case in the region it comes from.  |

That trade is the row's actual content.  |
|  **任课教师工作台 (Subject Teacher Workbench)**  |  [cyberdaddyleo/workstation-subject-teacher](https://github.com/cyberdaddyleo/workstation-subject-teacher)  |  🔴 **CUSTOM NON-COMMERCIAL — DO NOT SHIP.** `LICENSE`, **`2,351 B`**, sha256₁₂ `147601097b6a`, © *2026 赛博老爸*. A bespoke Chinese-language grant that **permits** personal and in-school use, source modification and free redistribution, and **forbids**, without prior written permission: selling the software or a modified version (*including renamed, reskinned or repackaged*), bundling it into any paid product, paid course, paid service or membership tier, building a **paid online service** on it, and *"any other use or distribution for profit"*. It also requires the `Developed by @赛博老爸` attribution to remain in the UI. **GitHub reports `NOASSERTION`.**  |  `main`  |  Single-file HTML; data stored locally in the browser  |  🆕 **New at p701, `33★`, placed **APAC**, and filed precisely so that nobody reaches for it.** A working teacher's workbench: multi-class timetables and substitutions, teaching progress, homework registration, single-subject and parallel-class grade analysis, drag-to-reseat, and an AI teaching assistant. 🔴 **This is the most dangerous licence shape this base holds.** An automated gate reports `NOASSERTION` — *unknown* — which reads as "needs review", not as "commercial use is prohibited"; and the prohibition is written in Chinese, so an English-language review may never resolve it. 🟢 **Keep it as a functional specification.** It is the clearest existing statement of what a Chinese subject teacher's daily workload actually contains, and that requirement list is reusable even though the code is not. See `T139`.  |
|  **吟诗 (Yinshi)**  |  [TTNAN/yinshi](https://github.com/TTNAN/yinshi)  |  🟡 **MIT + third-party annex** — `LICENSE`, **`3,443 B`**, sha256₁₂ `fa4f6554bf04`, © *2026 Caesar (TTNAN)*. A **verbatim MIT body followed by a long attribution annex**: bundled webfonts under **SIL OFL-1.1** (Noto Serif, Zhuque Fangsong, with OFL texts shipped in-tree), further CJK typefaces loaded at runtime from jsDelivr and `fonts.googleapis.cn`, Natural Earth river/lake data (public domain) and public-domain poem texts. **GitHub reports `NOASSERTION`** — correctly, because the body is not verbatim-standard once the annex is appended  |  `main`  |  Python; static site; poem corpus fetched at runtime  |  🆕 **New at p701, `72★`, placed **APAC** on named national content.** A classical-Chinese poetry reader that **auto-annotates poems with pinyin**, in a xuan-paper ink aesthetic, searchable by title, poet or line, with horizontal, vertical and 田字格 (grid-practice) layouts — and **polyphonic characters corrected to the readings in China's 统编 unified language textbook**, with per-character override. 🟢 **That textbook alignment is the placement evidence and the product differentiator at once**: pinyin for a polyphone is only "right" relative to a specific curriculum. 🔴 **Two obligations a licence gate will miss.** The OFL-1.1 fonts carry reserved-font-name conditions that MIT does not; and **the poem corpus is not in the repository at all** — it is fetched at runtime from two third-party public HTTP APIs, so the application's core content is an external dependency with no availability guarantee. A client deployment must mirror the corpus. See `T140`.  |
