| **Madrasa** | [quietbytehq/madrasa](https://github.com/quietbytehq/madrasa) | 🟢 **Apache-2.0** (`LICENSE`, `11,357 B`, sha256₁₂ `c71d239df917`) | `main` | Tauri 2 (Rust) desktop shell · React + TypeScript + Vite + Tailwind · Axum REST `/api/v1` · PostgreSQL with `sqlx` migrations · Argon2id + AES-GCM | 🆕 **p703 — a school information system whose MULTI-TENANCY IS IN THE DATA MODEL BEFORE THE FEATURES ARE.** Students, attendance, examinations, fees and finance, teachers, reports and backups, offline-capable for Linux and Windows. Three editions are planned — **Lite** (one school, ~1,200 students, all-in-one desktop install with a bundled database), **Standard** (server plus clients) and **Nexus** (a network hub joining several schools into one tenant, "like an Active Directory forest root") — and 🔵 **Lite is built first with UUID v7 keys, a `school_id` on every school-owned row, sync-ready columns and edition feature flags, so Standard and Nexus plug in without a rewrite.** That is the single decision that separates a school tool from a ministry-scale platform, and it is made here at pre-alpha. 🔴 **Status is `pre-alpha` and the author states plainly that nothing in it is ready for a real school** — filed for the data model and the editioning strategy, not for deployment. |
| **Timewizer** | [quietbytehq/timewizer](https://github.com/quietbytehq/timewizer) | 🟢 **Apache-2.0** (`LICENSE`, `11,358 B`, sha256₁₂ `cfc7749b96f6`, canonical text) | `main` | Desktop solver, Windows and Linux installers · one file per school · no login, no server | 🆕 **p703 — timetabling, and it is the first row on this shelf to address the hardest recurring OPERATIONS problem in a school.** An offline timetable generator for a single school: rules and limits go in, a solver builds the timetable, and the result is viewed per teacher, per class or per room. One file per school, no account, no network. 🔵 **Positioned by its author as an open-source alternative to aSc Timetables** — the proprietary product that dominates school timetabling across Central and Eastern Europe and much of Asia — which makes it the rare permissive artefact with a named commercial incumbent to displace. **Why it matters for AI work specifically: timetabling is a constraint-satisfaction problem, not a language problem**, so this is the solver an AI assistant should *call* rather than attempt, and the composition (agent collects the constraints in natural language, solver produces the timetable) is the pattern — see [`../compose/patterns.md`](../compose/patterns.md) `P92`. `0★`, same estate as **Madrasa** above. |
| **Bahar School Management System** | [AliAsgharPZ/school-management-system](https://github.com/AliAsgharPZ/school-management-system) | 🟢 **MIT** (`LICENSE`, `1,075 B`, sha256₁₂ `d8a16052aea0`) | `main` | Multi-tenant web SIS · attendance · examinations · fees · reports | 🆕 **p703 — a free multi-tenant student information system, filed as the MIT point of comparison to this shelf's AGPL incumbents.** Students, attendance, examinations, fees, staff and reports with tenant separation. 🔵 **Its value on this shelf is licence position, stated plainly**: the mature school-administration platforms this base already holds are overwhelmingly AGPL-3.0 or GPL-3.0, which forecloses a closed derivative, and this is one of the few MIT systems with a multi-tenant data model rather than a single-school one. `1★` and no external review, so it is a starting point to read and fork, not a product to recommend — but the licence makes forking it an option the copyleft shelf does not offer. |
| **AI Education ERP (multi-tenant SaaS)** | [imranbru99/school-and-college-management-erp-education-saas](https://github.com/imranbru99/school-and-college-management-erp-education-saas) | 🟢 **MIT** (`LICENSE`, `1,069 B`, sha256₁₂ `e1874ed3c9da`) | `main` | Laravel 12 · Filament admin · multi-tenant · schools and colleges | 🆕 **p703 — the ERP slot, occupied under MIT, for both schools AND colleges.** A multi-tenant education ERP and SaaS platform covering school and college administration on Laravel 12 with a Filament admin layer. 🔵 **Why the stack matters more than the star count: Laravel plus Filament is the fastest-to-customise admin stack in the PHP world and the one with the deepest contractor supply in South Asia, Africa and LATAM** — so for a client whose constraint is local maintainability after handover rather than engineering elegance, this is the honest recommendation over a Rust or Go system nobody nearby can maintain. 🔴 `0★`, no external review, and the "AI" in its own title is thin — treat the AI layer as something to add, and the tenancy and admin scaffolding as what you are actually adopting. Compare **OpenEduCat** on this shelf, which is Odoo-based and whose licence position is the reason this row exists. |
| **UniSphere Portal** | [YusufRashi/unisphere-portal](https://github.com/YusufRashi/unisphere-portal) | 🟢 **MIT** (`LICENSE`, `1,069 B`, sha256₁₂ `beface0e2308`) | `main` | Full-stack university portal · course registration · marks and GPA · PDF transcripts | 🆕 **p703 — higher education, and the two features it gets right are the two that actually break.** A university portal with **course registration carrying prerequisite and clash checks**, marks and GPA computation, and PDF transcript generation. 🔵 **Prerequisite and timetable-clash validation at registration time is the single most error-prone rule set in a student information system** — it is where home-grown university portals fail and where a term's enrolment data goes wrong — and finding it implemented under MIT in a readable codebase is worth more than the `0★` suggests. Filed as a reference implementation of registration rules, not as a platform. |
| **Agentic LMS (GenAIwithMS)** | [GenAIwithMS/LMS](https://github.com/GenAIwithMS/LMS) | 🟢 **MIT** (`LICENSE`, `1,072 B`, sha256₁₂ `398ca991615f`) | `main` | Modern web LMS · role-based access · agentic chatbot built in | 🆕 **p703 — an LMS with the agent inside the ROLE MODEL rather than bolted to the front end.** A learning management system with role-based access and an agentic chatbot as a first-class component. 🔵 **Filed because of where the agent sits: every LMS-plus-AI integration this base holds attaches a chat widget to an LMS that knows nothing about it, so the agent cannot be told what a student is permitted to see.** Here the roles and the agent are designed together, which is the precondition for an assistant that can answer "what is my grade" without leaking a classmate's. `4★`, early, and small enough to read end to end — which is the point of a reference. |

Four-stage methodology — demystify, command with the PACIF prompt framework, audit bias, build assistants — with testimonials from educators in the Guamá, Terra Firme and Icoaraci neighbourhoods. 🔵 **The deployment detail is the finding and it is the opposite of what an AI course usually assumes: a hub of four printable A4 study guides, built so a learner with a phone and no data allowance can study the material on paper.** Filed as the template for publicly-funded AI literacy at community scale; the artefact is a course platform, not an agent. Default branch is **`master`**. |

The ENEM *redação* is marked against five published competencies and sat by millions of candidates a year, which makes it the most valuable rubric in Brazilian education to automate against — and the most consequential to get wrong. 🔵 **Filed as a LATAM demand marker with its limits stated: `1★`, early, and the corrector's alignment to the five official competencies is not evidenced in the repository.** The reason to hold it is that it names the exam; the reason not to deploy it is that nothing here measures agreement with a human marker. Compose with **assessment-bench** and **bench-floor-audit** on [`../repos/foundations.md`](../repos/foundations.md) before anyone trusts a score. |
| **Conta Comigo** | [thyfanixs/conta-comigo](https://github.com/thyfanixs/conta-comigo) | 🟢 **MIT** (`LICENSE`, `1,071 B`, sha256₁₂ `de13aaebba81`) | `main` | Browser game · in-browser computer vision (hand/finger detection via webcam) · Portuguese | 🆕 **p703 — LATAM, and the input device is the child's HAND, with no hardware and no data leaving the browser.** A Portuguese-language arithmetic game for young children who answer by **showing fingers to the webcam**, with the counting recognised by computer vision running in the browser. 🔵 **Two reasons it earns a row despite `0★`: the interaction removes literacy and keyboard skill from the measurement, so it reaches pre-readers and early learners that every text-based tutor on these shelves excludes; and because the vision runs client-side, no image of a child is transmitted — which is the only posture that survives a children's-privacy review (COPPA in the US, the LGPD's children's provisions in Brazil).** Filed as an interaction pattern for early-years numeracy, not as a platform. |
| **OpenPath** | [`balejosg/OpenPath`](https://github.com/balejosg/OpenPath) | 🔴 **AGPL-3.0-or-later — network copyleft** (`LICENSE`, **`34,523 B`**, sha256₁₂ `8486a10c4393`, read from the bytes at `main`; `spdx_id` `AGPL-3.0` agrees) | `main` | Self-hosted classroom network service, CI + codecov gated | 🆕 **New at p709 — *“Intentional internet for the classroom. Private by design.”* `9★`, the most adopted thing p709 found anywhere.** Governs what a classroom's machines may reach, and its README argues against the content-filter framing it would normally be grouped with (*“no more cat and mouse”*) — i.e. **classroom attention management as infrastructure**, a vertical this shelf had no row for. Placed **EMEA** (Spain) on the owner's named Comunidad de Madrid corpus and FP-qualification repositories, the same owner-evidence chain that places [`balejosg/tiza`](https://github.com/balejosg/tiza) on [`../agents/top.md`](../agents/top.md). 🔴 **AGPL is the operative constraint, not a footnote:** if Globant hosts a modified OpenPath *as a service* for a school, **§13 obliges it to offer the modified source to the users of that service.** Deployable unmodified for a client, and modifiable for a client who self-hosts; **not** foldable into a proprietary managed offering. **Read this row together with the one below it — they are the same problem, the same owner, and two deliberately different grants.** |
| **ClassroomPath** | [`balejosg/ClassroomPath`](https://github.com/balejosg/ClassroomPath) | 🔴 **NOT OPEN SOURCE — “ClassroomPath Source-Available License 1.0”** (`LICENSE`, `4,137 B`, sha256₁₂ `56f9919c5628`, read from the bytes at `main`; GitHub reports `spdx_id` **`NOASSERTION`**) | `main` | Managed classroom internet access over an auditable open-source core | 🆕 **New at p709, and filed PRECISELY SO IT IS NOT MISTAKEN FOR AVAILABLE.** The grant says so in its own second sentence — *“This license is source-available, not open source”* — and then enumerates it: the rights granted are read, audit, **modify privately**, and run **only in private non-production development or test environments**; `Production Use` is defined to include *“pilots, school deployments, hosted instances, managed services, and internal institutional rollouts”*, and `Reproduction` — deploying, hosting, operating, distributing, sublicensing, selling, leasing, white-labeling, or offering as a service — **requires prior written permission.** For a studio that means: **usable for procurement evaluation and security review, unusable for any client engagement, including an internal pilot.** 🟢 **Why it earns a row anyway — the pair is the lesson.** The same owner publishes [`balejosg/OpenPath`](https://github.com/balejosg/OpenPath) (AGPL-3.0, `9★`) and this (`0★`) over the same problem, and this licence even defines *“OpenPath Components”* as files that **stay under their own notices** — so the boundary is drawn in the text, not inferred. **A textbook open-core split, captured on both sides at the moment it was drawn** (both pushed `2026-11-05`). 🔴 **And the transferable warning: `NOASSERTION` does not mean “unknown, probably permissive”.** Here it means a bespoke proprietary grant that a pipeline keying on `spdx_id` would carry as missing metadata rather than as a prohibition — which is how a source-available product ends up in a client proposal. **`4,137 B` of licence had to be read to find that out.** |
| **SkillBridge** | [`Shivam7977/SkillBridge-Platform`](https://github.com/Shivam7977/SkillBridge-Platform) | 🟢 **MIT** (`LICENSE`, `1,076 B`, sha256₁₂ `8ff76a8af93f`, read from the bytes at `main`) | `main` | Next.js-era web stack; AI roadmap generator, resume/portfolio template engine (19 resume + 6 portfolio templates), mock-interview room with XP/badge gamification | 🆕 **New at p710 — and it is the first row on this shelf for the *employability* vertical rather than the instruction vertical.** A unified learning-and-career platform for students: generates **AI learning roadmaps for a target role** with free resources per stage, builds resumes and portfolios from templates, and runs an **adaptive mock-interview room** with gamified progression and a project community. `5★`, pushed **2026-11-05**, alive at `main` HEAD `422c3f98f8c7`. 🟢 **Why it earns a verticals row at `5★`:** every other platform on this shelf organises around a *course*, and the thing a government skilling programme or a bootcamp actually buys is organised around a **job outcome** — roadmap, artefact, interview, placement. This is the only permissive system on this shelf shaped that way, so it is the natural base for a workforce-development engagement. 🔵 **Composes directly with this pass's foundations rows:** the `ai-infra-curriculum` ladder supplies the role-by-role content the roadmap generator otherwise invents. 🟡 Single-author, no release, no deployment guide; adopt as a source tree. |
| **Plato** | [`1111philo/plato`](https://github.com/1111philo/plato) | 🔴 **AGPL-3.0 — copyleft, and GitHub's own metadata does NOT say so** (`LICENSE`, **`35,253 B`**, sha256₁₂ `e415aafbff89`, read from the bytes at `main`; payload is the GNU Affero text) | `main` | — (self-described adaptive learning platform; stack not documented in-repo) | 🆕 **New at p710, and it is filed as a WARNING with a measurement attached.** Self-described *“Adaptive Learning Platform — Open Source — Made for Humans — Responsibly Using AI”*, `24★`, pushed **2026-11-07**, alive at `main` HEAD `f88bcefacfcf`. 🔴 **`gh api search/repositories` reports its `license.spdx_id` as `NOASSERTION`; the payload at `main` is `35,253 B` of GNU **Affero** General Public License.** A pipeline that trusts `spdx_id` sees *“no licence information”* and a careless reader resolves that to *“probably permissive, ask later”* — **the actual grant is the strongest copyleft in common use, and for a hosted education platform the Affero clause is the operative one: offering it over a network to students obliges you to offer them the modified source.** 🟢 **This is the fourth `NOASSERTION` divergence this base has resolved by reading bytes** (after `ClassroomPath`'s source-available licence at `p709`, and the two at `695-C`/`681-B`), and it is the first where the hidden grant is AGPL rather than a bespoke or absent one. **The shelf rule from `p709` holds and is now better evidenced: `NOASSERTION` means UNREAD, never unrestricted.** 🔴 **Scope consequence:** do not propose Plato as a base for a client-hosted platform without a commercial-licensing conversation with its authors. Filed so the `24★` and the attractive description do not cost another pass the same measurement. |
| **LTI AI grader (UPV)** | [`moocupv/lti-ai-grader`](https://github.com/moocupv/lti-ai-grader) | 🟢 **Apache-2.0** (`LICENSE`, `11,357 B`, sha256₁₂ `c71d239df917`, read from the bytes at `main`) — **byte-identical to the `apache/.github` canonical this base pinned at `p677`**, so the payload needs no further adjudication | `main` | LTI 1.1 over CGI-bin · Gemini or any OpenAI-compatible API · configurable prompt per activity | 🆕 **New at p712, and it is the only row on this shelf that serves TWO platform families with ONE artifact:** a single LTI tool that sends a learner's answer to an LLM and writes the grade back into **Open edX *and* Moodle** ("Tested with Open edx and Moodle"). Every other integration row on this shelf is bound to one family. 🔵 **Placed EMEA from the artifact:** the UI runs in English, Spanish and **Valencian** — a co-official language of one Spanish autonomous community and therefore a sharper locator than "Spanish" ever is — the Open edX example host is `groq.cc.upv.es` and the example model is `poligpt-code`, which is Universitat Politècnica de València's own deployment. ⚠ **Two version claims that disagree:** the repository's About line says *LTI 1.0* and the README says *LTI 1.1*. LTI 1.0 and 1.1 differ in whether grade pass-back exists at all, so this must be resolved against the code before an engagement depends on it. |
| **Brightspace tenant extension (Scheduler)** | [`mattdig/brightspace-scheduler`](https://github.com/mattdig/brightspace-scheduler) | 🟢 **MIT** (`LICENSE`, `1,064 B`, sha256₁₂ `0e23d466aa5a`, read from the bytes at `main`; holder *"Copyright (c) 2022 mattdig"*) | `main` | static JS/HTML uploaded to Brightspace "Public Files" · two Custom Links in the navbar · self-enrolled groups + the course calendar as the datastore | 🆕 **New at p712, and it contains NO AI — which is why it is on this shelf and not the agent shelf.** It is the answer to "how do you customise a Brightspace tenant when D2L gives you no plugin API": upload static files to the Public Files area, wire two Custom Links (one instructor-facing to create schedules, one student-facing to sign up), and let self-enrolled groups plus the calendar carry the state. 🔵 **Filed as the attachment point.** Every Brightspace agent filed at p712 *reads* the tenant through a borrowed user session; this is the only artifact that *adds a feature to the tenant itself*, so it is where an AI feature would attach for a D2L client. ⚠ It assumes students can see Class Lists; without that permission it still runs with reduced function. The `2022` holder line makes it the oldest artifact in this pass's field. |
| **Open edX agent configuration (Unicon)** | [`Unicon/unicon-openedx-ai-config`](https://github.com/Unicon/unicon-openedx-ai-config) | 🟢 **Apache-2.0** (`LICENSE`, `11,358 B`, sha256₁₂ `cfc7749b96f6`, read from the bytes at `main`) — **byte-identical to the `apache.org` canonical this base pinned at `p677`** | `main` | Claude Code + Codex configuration · local dev setup notes · deployment notes · planning docs | 🆕 **New at p712, and it is the only row on this shelf that is an ENGINEERING asset rather than a product.** An Open edX service provider's version-controlled configuration for coding agents working on Open edX — it "started as config for AI coding assistants working on Open edX" and became the home for everything the firm wants under version control that does not belong upstream, including how to run Claude Code and Codex together on the same codebase. 🔵 **Why a studio should read it before an Open edX engagement:** it is a competitor's published answer to "how do you point coding agents at a large AGPL platform you do not own", which is the first problem of any Open edX project and is nowhere in Open edX's own documentation. 🟡 **Region left `Global` deliberately:** neither the artifact nor the owner's declared profile names a country, and this shelf does not place a row on a firm's reputation (`712-A`, channel 4 nil). |
| **AI Verify** | [`aiverify-foundation/aiverify`](https://github.com/aiverify-foundation/aiverify) | 🟢 **Apache-2.0** (`LICENSE`, `11,350 B`, sha256₁₂ `63a6b58f3c9a`, read from the bytes at `main`; holder *“Copyright 2023 AI Verify Foundation”*) | `main` | Python + TypeScript, plugin architecture | 🆕 **New at p713, and it is the first row on this shelf that is a REGULATOR'S OWN INSTRUMENT rather than a vendor's (`713-G`).** AI Verify is Singapore's AI governance **testing framework and toolkit**, stewarded by the AI Verify Foundation under IMDA — the same toolkit the APAC regulatory leg named in prose, which turned out to be on GitHub under Apache-2.0. 🟢 **That inverts the standing read of the mandated regional legs.** `703-A` and `704-A` concluded they are a *market* instrument that returns no supply; here a regional leg named an instrument and the instrument resolved to a permissive repository this base can ship. **The leg's output was a product name, not a repository address — so the nil was in the EXTRACTION, not in the leg.** 🟢 **As a platform it is a customisation surface**: tests are plugins, so a *"show the ministry how this tutor was evaluated"* engagement is a plugin-authoring job on an Apache-2.0 base (`P114`), not a bespoke build. 🔵 README announces **AI Verify 2.0** as upcoming — re-read this row next pass. Pushed `2026-11-03`, only `6` open issues. `106★`. |
| **DSpace (Angular UI)** | [`DSpace/dspace-angular`](https://github.com/DSpace/dspace-angular) | 🟢 **BSD-3-Clause** (`LICENSE`, `1,504 B`, sha256₁₂ `baf9ee18d437`, read from the bytes at `main`; holder *“Copyright (c) 2002-2021, LYRASIS. All rights reserved.”* — 🔵 **a `24`-year copyright range and a named non-profit steward**, which is the strongest institutional-continuity signal on this shelf) | `main` | Angular + TypeScript (REST client to the Java backend) | 🆕 **New at p713 — and it opens a SEGMENT this shelf did not cover: the institutional repository.** DSpace is the dominant open-source platform for university research repositories and theses; this is its Angular user interface. 🟢 **The AI read is that every institution running DSpace is already sitting on a curated, rights-cleared, metadata-rich corpus of its OWN scholarship** — which is the single best RAG source on campus and the one with the fewest copyright objections. Wire it to `HKUDS/LightRAG` and the retrieval layer is grounded in material the institution already owns the rights to (`P114`'s evidence leg). 🟢 **BSD-3-Clause is the most permissive licence on this shelf** — more so than the Apache rows, with no patent grant to reason about. 🔴 `597` open issues and a separate Java backend (`DSpace/DSpace`) that this row does NOT cover; the backend's licence must be read on its own before any deployment claim. Pushed `2026-11-06`. `178★`. |


### 🟡 `725-G` — the pesantren/madrasah segment is real, sizeable and almost entirely unlicensed

Indonesia's Islamic boarding schools and madrasahs are an administration market no pass of this
base had placed. A single-term leg (`madrasah OR pesantren`, `stars:>3`) returned the segment in
one query, and the licence distribution is the finding:

| Repo | Stars | Licence (payload-read) | Stageable |
|---|--:|---|---|
| [`dibaliqaja/pesantren-cms`](https://github.com/dibaliqaja/pesantren-cms) | 38 | **none — 4 / 4 filenames `404`** | 🔴 No |
| [`AhmadMuzayyin/digitren`](https://github.com/AhmadMuzayyin/digitren) | 14 | 🟢 **MIT**, verbatim | 🟡 Yes, but stale `2025-11-22` |
| [`syauqi357/siakad-madrasah`](https://github.com/syauqi357/siakad-madrasah) | 10 | **none — 4 / 4 filenames `404`** | 🔴 No |

🔵 **The two largest repositories in the segment carry no grant, and the only permissive one is the
smallest and the least maintained.** So for an APAC engagement in this segment the honest posture is
*build on a generic stack and bring the domain model* — `digitren`'s schema is the reference for
santri records, finance and staff — rather than *adopt a platform*. 🟡 This is the same shape as
`724-C`'s permissive-ERP gap, one tier further down: where the install base is, the licence is not.

**Licence method, this pass.** Every new row above was confirmed from the **payload**, not from the
index: `LICENSE` title windows read `MIT License` (`digitren`), `GNU AFFERO GENERAL PUBLIC LICENSE
Version 3` (`giuaschool`), and for the two nil rows all of `LICENSE`, `LICENSE.md`, `LICENSE.txt` and
`COPYING` returned `404`. **`5 / 5` payload reads agreed with the index field this pass** — worth
recording, because the recent history on this page is of index fields that did not (`724-L`, `722-L`,
Sakai's ECL-2.0). The index being right is a measurement, not an assumption.
